#### D-09.2.3 — Sub-SO for CRA

```yaml
id: SO-D-09.2.CRA
subdomain: D-09.2
applies_to: [CRA]
inherits_from: SO-CRA-053 + SO-CRA-054
source_SR: SR-CRA-064 + SR-CRA-065 + SR-CRA-066 + SR-CRA-079 + SR-CRA-080 + SR-CRA-081
activation: if CRA in applicable_regs
phase_1A_role: 1_of_5
verified_relationship: COMPLEMENTARY (CRDA-deep, D-09.2: GDPR↔CRA = DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE (SAME — pre-launch DPIA on data-subject rights vs pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment on product cybersecurity risk; **no OJ-level consolidation rule** even when CRA risk assessment performed); NIS2↔CRA = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — entity-level operational + product-level pre-market; meet at supplier-customer interface); CRA↔DORA = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — CRA product-level pre-market + DORA entity-level operational; Conditional-Y scope overlap); **CRA↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME) — CRITICAL CONSOLIDATION RULE**: CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 explicitly permits the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); **only OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in D-09.2**)
```

**Objective.** The manufacturer documents the **cybersecurity risk assessment referred to in Art. 13(2)** and updates it as appropriate during the support period, with the assessment covering intended purpose and reasonably foreseeable use (Art. 13(3) sentence 2) and indicating Annex I Part I (2) applicability (Art. 13(3) sentence 3) — documented in the **Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record** (Art. 13(4) sentence 1 + Annex VII §3 CRA).

**CSF anchors (from `SR-CRA-064` + `SR-CRA-065` + `SR-CRA-066` + `SR-CRA-079` v0.3 security_rationale):**
ID.RA-05 (primary), ID.SC-04.
*(ID.RA-05 — Threats, vulnerabilities, likelihoods, impacts used to understand inherent risk and inform risk response — anchors the CRA Art. 13(3) documented-and-updated cybersecurity risk assessment mapped to NIST CSF 2.0 ID.RA-05. ID.SC-04 — Suppliers prioritised by criticality — anchors the supply-chain risk dimension per DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 180 cross-validation. The CRA risk assessment covers (1) intended purpose + reasonably foreseeable use (including operational environment and assets to be protected) per Art. 13(3) sentence 2; (2) Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping per Art. 13(3) sentence 3; (3) technical-documentation inclusion per Art. 13(4) sentence 1; (4) Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record per the documentation requirement. The **CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule** permits the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for the CRA cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product — for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products, a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system discharges both CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment and AI Act Art. 9 risk management on a single artefact (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes). The two subcategories together enable the manufacturer to demonstrate ex post, against the market surveillance authority, that the Art. 13(3) sentence 1 documented-and-updated risk assessment was carried out with Annex VII §3 documentation, Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping, and updated on material change of risk per Art. 13(3) sentence 1 `updated as appropriate` + Art. 13(7) update-on-becoming-aware-of-vulnerability. **Note — CRA risk assessment does NOT extend to data-subject rights**: per CRDA flag (DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 175), the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the CRA cybersecurity risk assessment has been performed.)*

#### D-09.2.4 — Sub-SO for DORA

```yaml
id: SO-D-09.2.DORA
subdomain: D-09.2
applies_to: [DORA]
inherits_from: SO-DORA-017 + SO-DORA-003
source_SR: SR-DORA-028 + SR-DORA-004 + SR-DORA-005
activation: if DORA in applicable_regs
phase_1A_role: 1_of_5
verified_relationship: DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE / COMPLEMENTARY / EQUAL (CRDA-deep, D-09.2: GDPR↔DORA = DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE (SAME — pre-launch DPIA vs continuous + at-least-yearly DORA risk framework; **no OJ-level consolidation**); NIS2↔DORA = EQUAL (SAME — both regulations impose continuous operational risk-identification obligations; DORA-dominant for financial entities under DORA's direct scope; EQUAL on continuous operational risk identification); CRA↔DORA = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — CRA product-level pre-market + DORA entity-level operational; Conditional-Y scope overlap when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant); DORA↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — DORA entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; **no OJ-level consolidation** — DORA has no equivalent to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2))
```

**Objective.** Financial entities shall, on a continuous basis, **identify all sources of ICT risk** including risk exposure to and from other financial entities, and **assess cyber threats and ICT vulnerabilities relevant to ICT supported business functions, information assets and ICT assets** (Art. 7(2) sentence 1 DORA); risk scenarios are reviewed on a regular basis, and at least yearly (Art. 7(2) sentence 2), under a three-lines-of-defence model (Art. 6(4) DORA).

**CSF anchors (from `SR-DORA-028` + `SR-DORA-004` + `SR-DORA-005` v0.3 security_rationale):**
ID.RA-05 (primary), GV.RR-02.
*(ID.RA-05 — Threats, vulnerabilities, likelihoods, impacts used to understand inherent risk and inform risk response — anchors the DORA Art. 7(2) sentence 1 continuous ICT risk identification + sentence 2 at-least-yearly risk-scenario review mapped to NIST CSF 2.0 ID.RA-05. The three-lines-of-defence model (Art. 6(4)) provides the governance architecture for the ICT risk identification + asset classification + risk-scenario review chain. The two subcategories together enable the financial entity to demonstrate ex post, against the national competent authority + EBA Guidelines on ICT and security risk management supervisory baseline, that the Art. 7(2) sentence 1 continuous ICT risk identification register + sentence 2 at-least-yearly risk-scenario review records + Art. 6(4) three-lines-of-defence documentation + Art. 7(1) asset identification/classification/documentation chain are maintained on the entity-level operational continuous + at-least-yearly cadence per Art. 4 proportionality. **Note — DORA ICT risk framework does NOT extend to data-subject rights**: per CRDA flag (DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 175), the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the DORA ICT risk framework is in place; **DORA has no equivalent to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2** — DORA ↔ AI Act pair has no OJ-level consolidation rule. **Note — DORA OJ-literal locus**: the OJ-literal locus for Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + sentence 2 is Art. 8(2) per the SR-DORA-004 / SR-DORA-005 v0.2 audit note; the SR preserves the substantive content of the risk-identification-on-a-continuous-basis + at-least-yearly risk-scenario review obligation.)*

#### D-09.2.5 — Sub-SO for AI Act

```yaml
id: SO-D-09.2.AI_Act
subdomain: D-09.2
applies_to: [AI_Act]
inherits_from: SO-AIACT-001
source_SR: SR-AIACT-001 + SR-AIACT-002
activation: if AI_Act in applicable_regs
phase_1A_role: 1_of_5
verified_relationship: DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE / COMPLEMENTARY (CRDA-deep, D-09.2: GDPR↔AI_Act = DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE (SAME — pre-launch DPIA on data-subject rights vs continuous iterative AI Act Art. 9 risk management; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; **no OJ-level consolidation**); NIS2↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — NIS 2 entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative); **CRA↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME) — CRITICAL CONSOLIDATION RULE**: CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule permits the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product; **only OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in D-09.2**; DORA↔AI_Act = COMPLEMENTARY (SAME — DORA entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; **no OJ-level consolidation**))
```

**Objective.** A risk management system is **established, implemented, documented and maintained** in relation to high-risk AI systems (Art. 9(1) AI Act), comprising the four Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) steps: identify and analyse known and reasonably foreseeable risks; estimate and evaluate reasonably-foreseeable-misuse risks; evaluate post-market-monitoring-data risks; and adopt appropriate and targeted risk-management measures.

**CSF anchors (from `SR-AIACT-001` + `SR-AIACT-002` v0.3 security_rationale):**
ID.RA-05 (primary), GV.RM-04.
*(ID.RA-05 — Threats, vulnerabilities, likelihoods, impacts used to understand inherent risk and inform risk response — anchors the AI Act Art. 9(1) + Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) continuous iterative risk-management system across AI lifecycle mapped to NIST CSF 2.0 ID.RA-05. GV.RM-04 — Strategic direction for risk response — anchors the continuous-iterative dimension per DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 180 cross-validation. The 4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) process — (a) identify/analyse known + reasonably foreseeable risks to health, safety and fundamental rights; (b) estimate/evaluate reasonably-foreseeable-misuse risks; (c) evaluate post-market-monitoring-data risks; (d) adopt appropriate and targeted risk-management measures — defines the AI-system-level continuous iterative assessment methodology. **CRITICAL CONSOLIDATION RULE — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification**: for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products, a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); this is the only OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in D-09.2. The two subcategories together enable the AI Act provider / deployer to demonstrate ex post, against the MSA / AI Office + Art. 27 FRIA documentation for certain Annex III categories, that the Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` risk-management system was carried out across the AI lifecycle with the 4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) process documented and applied to the high-risk AI system, with post-market monitoring integrated per Art. 9(2)(c) and reasonable foreseeability scoping per Art. 9(2)(a) + (b). **Note — AI Act risk management does NOT address data-subject rights**: per CRDA flag (DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 175), the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system is in place; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation.)*

---

#### Emergent tensions (from CRDA-deep §D-09.2)

**0 conditional emergent tensions** on the impact-and-risk-assessment duty itself — no pair was verified as CONTRADICTORY or CORRECTED. The 10 pairs are all SAME — 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE (all 4 GDPR pairs) + 5 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY + 1 SAME — EQUAL (NIS 2 ↔ DORA on continuous operational risk identification). **No scope-disjoint pairs** at D-09.2 — every pair activates at the Y or Conditional-Y-on-same-party level. **No SCOPE-DISJOINT reclassifications** — CRDA preserves every pair as SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE / COMPLEMENTARY / EQUAL. **No CONTRADICTORY pairs** on the assessment-object dimension itself. The **assessment-object divergence** (data-subject rights / entity operational risk / product cybersecurity risk / ICT operational risk / AI-system risk) is the **legitimately diverse dimension**: each regulator operates on a distinct assessment object with a distinct trigger and lifecycle phase, and the operational convergence is on a **layered 5-assessment architecture** with 5 distinct cadences — pre-launch DPIA / continuous operational / pre-market + support-period / continuous + at-least-yearly / continuous iterative across AI lifecycle. The high-level SO surfaces the common documented-assessment duty (documented assessment + identified risks + mitigation measures + review cadence + per-reg-object preservation); the per-reg assessment object, trigger, content template, threshold, recipient, and review cadence are documented separately in the 5 sub-SOs and the per-recipient evidence packs.

The **single OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule** (CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification) is the structurally significant operational harmonisation — the ONLY cross-regulation substitution rule at D-09.2: for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products (the SAME product is both CRA-regulated and AI-Act high-risk), a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes). **No other cross-regulation substitution is OJ-permitted** — the GDPR DPIA, NIS 2 risk-analysis policy, DORA ICT risk framework, and CRA risk assessment (when not subject to Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation) are all distinct artefacts with no OJ-level substitution rule. The 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) reflect the structural pre-launch DPIA vs continuous / iterative assessment asymmetry; **CRDA flag — GDPR DPIA does NOT address cybersecurity risk** (DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 175) — `GDPR Art. 35(7) covers risks to data-subject rights and freedoms; it does not require cybersecurity-risk analysis; for a CRA-regulated product processing personal data, the GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact even when the CRA risk assessment has been performed`. The 5 cadences are not synchronised and no OJ-level cadence harmonisation exists (DomainAnalysis §D-09.2 line 174 — pre-launch vs operational review asymmetry).

The **NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL classification on continuous operational risk identification** is the structurally significant EQUAL pair: both regulations impose continuous operational risk-identification obligations. NIS 2 Art. 21(2)(a) `policies on risk analysis and information system security` is entity-discretionary under Recital 56 proportionality + state-of-the-art floor per Art. 21(1). DORA Art. 6(4) three-lines-of-defence + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 `continuously … identify all sources of ICT risk` + sentence 2 `review on a regular basis, and at least yearly, the risk scenarios` is the dedicated financial-entity operational framework. For DORA financial entities under DORA's direct scope, the DORA Art. 6(4) + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + sentence 2 discharges both DORA's dedicated risk-analysis obligations and NIS 2's Art. 21(2)(a) policy artefacts for DORA financial entities. For non-financial NSIEs, NIS 2 is the authority. The EQUAL classification preserves the OJ literal parallel between the two regimes on the continuous operational risk-identification dimension; the dominant-authority reading is the operational consequence of the DORA-direct-applicability vs NIS 2-MS-transposition asymmetry. **NOTE — DORA OJ-literal locus**: the OJ-literal locus for Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + sentence 2 is Art. 8(2) per the SR-DORA-004 / SR-DORA-005 v0.2 audit note; the SR preserves the substantive content.

The high-level SO surfaces the common risk-assessment duty (documented assessment + identified risks + mitigation measures + review cadence + per-reg-object preservation); the per-reg perspective on the assessment object, trigger, content template, threshold, recipient, review cadence, and evidence template is documented separately in the 5 sub-SOs and the per-recipient evidence packs. **0 conditional emergent tensions** because no pair was verified as CONTRADICTORY or CORRECTED — the layered 5-assessment architecture (data-subject rights / entity operational risk / product cybersecurity risk / ICT operational risk / AI-system risk) with 5 distinct assessment triggers and review cadences is itself the layered reconciliation that supersedes any single-regulator reading. **1 OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule** (CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2) operationalises the AI-enabled-CRA-regulated-product harmonisation on a single artefact.

#### Downstream implication for Phase 1C Doc 07

A company with ≥2 applicable regulations in scope of D-09.2 — with overlapping assessment-architecture scope-determiners on the same party — operates a **layered 5-assessment architecture** with 5 distinct assessment objects, 5 distinct triggers, 5 distinct content templates, 5 distinct review cadences, 5 distinct recipients. The high-level SO (`SO-D-09.2.HL`) describes the common documented-assessment duty (documented assessment + identified risks + mitigation measures + review cadence + per-reg-object preservation); the per-reg perspective is preserved in the 5 sub-SOs (assessment object, trigger, content template, threshold, recipient, review cadence, evidence template). **The 0 conditional emergent tensions are documented as a layered reconciliation**, with the 5-assessment architecture operationalising the 5 distinct assessment triggers and review cadences without a unified OJ-level cadence. The high-level SO does NOT collapse the 5 assessment regimes into a single artefact; the per-reg perspective on the assessment object, trigger, content template, threshold, recipient, review cadence, and evidence template is preserved verbatim. Phase 1C Doc 07 documents the **impact-and-risk-assessment strategy**: (1) a 5-layer assessment architecture with explicit assessment-object hand-offs (GDPR Art. 35(1) + Art. 35(7) + Art. 35(11) pre-launch DPIA on data-subject rights → NIS 2 Art. 21(2)(a) + SR-NIS2-028 + Art. 22(1) continuous operational risk-analysis policy on entity operational risk → CRA Art. 13(3) sentence 1 + sentence 2 + sentence 3 + Annex VII §3 pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment on product cybersecurity risk → DORA Art. 6(4) + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + sentence 2 continuous + at-least-yearly ICT risk framework on financial-entity ICT operational risk → AI Act Art. 9(1) + Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) + Art. 27 FRIA continuous iterative risk-management system on AI-system risk); (2) the single OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule (CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 — for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products, a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment) is a Phase 1C Doc 07 harmonisation item — for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products, the unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system discharges both obligations on a single artefact; (3) the Layer 1 cross-cutting assessment-objects mapping identifies the 5 distinct assessment *objects* (data-subject rights / entity operational risk / product cybersecurity risk / ICT operational risk / AI-system risk) and aligns the 5 distinct review cadences (pre-launch + on-change review / continuous / pre-market + support-period + on-material-change / continuous + at-least-yearly / continuous iterative across AI lifecycle) without a unified OJ-level cadence; (4) the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the CRA risk assessment / NIS 2 risk-analysis policy / DORA ICT risk framework / AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system is in place — the CRDA DomainAnalysis flag (`GDPR DPIA does not address cybersecurity risk`) is the operational interpretation; (5) the NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL classification on continuous operational risk identification discharges both obligations on the DORA-dominant stack for financial entities under DORA's direct scope — for non-financial NSIEs, NIS 2 is the authority; (6) the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) reflect the structural pre-launch DPIA vs continuous / iterative assessment asymmetry — the **CRITICAL CRDA DomainAnalysis flag** (line 173) `Five overlapping risk/impact assessments with only one consolidation rule: GDPR DPIA (Art. 35), NIS 2 risk analysis (Art. 21(2)(a) + Art. 22), CRA cybersecurity risk assessment (Art. 13(3) + Annex VII §3), DORA ICT risk framework (Art. 6/7), AI Act FRIA-equivalent (Art. 9) — five separate assessment regimes that may all apply to the same entity-product combination. The ONLY OJ-level consolidation rule is CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 (AI Act Art. 9 risk management substitutes for CRA risk assessment). No other cross-regulation consolidation exists. Phase 1 entities must maintain five separate risk registers with no OJ-level cross-referencing. **Recommend Layer 1 cross-cutting analysis** to map the five assessment objects` is the Layer 1 mapping basis. The high-level SO does NOT resolve any assessment-object contradiction because none exist by construction (10 SAME — 4 DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE + 5 COMPLEMENTARY + 1 EQUAL); the Layer 1 cross-cutting assessment-objects mapping is the operational mechanism for nesting the 5 assessment regimes in a single integrated assessment architecture, and the single OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule (CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2) is the operational mechanism for unifying the CRA ↔ AI Act assessments on a single artefact when both apply to the same product.

### 3. Security Requirements

## 9.2 Impact & Risk Assessments — Volere requirements

> **Provenance.** Derived from `../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md`. See that file for the CRDA-deep verified classification (10 pairs: 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE + 5 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY + 1 SAME — EQUAL — no CORRECTED, no CONTRADICTORY, no scope-disjoint pairs; **5 of 5 regulations participate**; **1 OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule** — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 permitting AI Act Art. 9 risk management to substitute for CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product), the 5 distinct assessment objects (data-subject rights / entity operational / product cybersecurity / ICT operational / AI-system), the 5 distinct review cadences (pre-launch on-change / continuous / pre-market + support-period / continuous + yearly / continuous iterative), the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs reflecting pre-launch DPIA vs operational-continuous asymmetry with **no OJ-level consolidation** — GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact even when CRA risk assessment has been performed per DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175 CRDA flag: "GDPR DPIA does NOT address cybersecurity risk"), the NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL on continuous operational risk identification (DORA-dominant for financial entities), the CRA ↔ AI_Act consolidation rule (CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 — the only OJ-permitted substitution in 9.2), the per-reg assessment object / trigger / content template / threshold / recipient / review cadence / legal anchor, and the aggregation logic. This file adds Volere shell fields per the `../Volere_shell.md` template.

### Participating regulations

GDPR, NIS 2, CRA, DORA, AI Act (ALL 5 — no scope-disjoint pair at 9.2, hence the only sub-domain where the HL engages whenever ≥2 regs are applicable; CRDA flag: "Five overlapping risk/impact assessments with only one consolidation rule"). Each regulator imposes a **distinct assessment regime** at a **distinct assessment object** under a **distinct trigger** at a **distinct lifecycle phase** with a **distinct review cadence**: GDPR (Art. 35(1) `prior to processing … carry out an assessment of the impact of the envisaged processing operations on the protection of personal data` + Art. 35(7) 4-item content + Art. 35(11) `review on change of risk`) — pre-launch DPIA on data-subject rights; NIS 2 (Art. 21(2)(a) `policies on risk analysis and information system security` + Art. 21(1) `appropriate and proportionate` chapeau + state-of-the-art floor per Art. 21(1) sentence 2 + Recital 56 cost factor + Art. 22(1) Cooperation Group coordinated assessments) — continuous operational risk-analysis policy on entity operational risk; CRA (Art. 13(3) sentence 1 `documented and updated` + sentence 2 `intended purpose and reasonably foreseeable use` + sentence 3 Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping + Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record) — pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment on product cybersecurity risk; DORA (Art. 6(4) `three lines of defence model` + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 `continuously … identify all sources of ICT risk` + sentence 2 `review on a regular basis, and at least yearly, the risk scenarios`) — continuous + at-least-yearly ICT risk framework on financial-entity ICT operational risk; AI Act (Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` + Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) 4-step process + Art. 27 FRIA for certain Annex III categories) — continuous iterative risk-management system on AI-system risk.

### High-level requirement

```yaml
- req_id: 9.2
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: integrity
  title: "Impact and risk assessments are established through a documented assessment of identified risks and mitigation measures with a review cadence calibrated to the lifecycle phase of the assessment object"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.2.HL
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.2.0"

  description: |
    The product shall establish impact and risk assessments through documented assessments of identified risks and mitigation measures with a review cadence calibrated to the lifecycle phase of the assessment object, where ≥2 of the participating regulations (GDPR, NIS 2, CRA, DORA, AI Act) have applicability with overlapping assessment-architecture scope-determiners on the same party, with the 5 distinct per-reg assessment objects, triggers, content templates, thresholds, recipients, and review cadences preserved verbatim in the 5 sub-SOs, and with the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 OJ-level consolidation rule permitting AI Act Art. 9 risk management to substitute for CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product.

  fit_criterion: |
    A documented impact-and-risk-assessment architecture is established covering all 5 participating assessment regimes (or the subset applicable to the entity) at the assessment-evidence layer: (1) GDPR pre-launch DPIA per Art. 35(1) `prior to processing … carry out an assessment of the impact of the envisaged processing operations on the protection of personal data` with the 4-item Art. 35(7) content (systematic description of processing + necessity/proportionality + risks to data-subject rights + measures envisaged to address risks) reviewed on change of risk per Art. 35(11) and submitted for prior consultation where residual risk is high per Art. 36(1), submitted to DPA on demand under Art. 5(2) accountability; (2) NIS 2 continuous operational risk-analysis policy per Art. 21(2)(a) `policies on risk analysis and information system security` with all-hazards methodology per SR-NIS2-040, calibrated by Art. 21(1) chapeau `appropriate and proportionate` + state-of-the-art floor + Recital 56 cost factor, incorporating Art. 22(1) Cooperation Group coordinated supply-chain assessments per Art. 21(3) sentence 2, management-body-approved per Art. 20(1); (3) CRA pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment per Art. 13(3) sentence 1 `documented and updated` covering intended purpose + reasonably foreseeable use per sentence 2 + Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping per sentence 3, documented in Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record, updated on material change of risk or upon becoming aware of a previously unidentified vulnerability per Art. 13(7), with Art. 27 presumption-of-conformity machinery for harmonised-standards-aligned assessments; (4) DORA continuous ICT risk identification register per Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + at-least-yearly risk-scenario review per sentence 2, under three-lines-of-defence per Art. 6(4), with proportionality calibrated to Art. 4 `size, nature, scale and complexity`; (5) AI Act continuous iterative risk-management system per Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` with the 4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) process documented and integrated with Art. 27 FRIA for certain Annex III categories; the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) discharge the GDPR DPIA as a **separate artefact** even when CRA / DORA / NIS 2 / AI Act assessments are in place (per DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175 CRDA flag — GDPR DPIA does NOT address cybersecurity risk), the NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL pair discharges both obligations on the DORA-dominant stack for financial entities, and the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule discharges CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment on a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system artefact for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes — the ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2).

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.2.1, 9.2.2, 9.2.3, 9.2.4, 9.2.5]

  nist_csf: [ID.RA-01, ID.RA-04, ID.RA-05]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [GDPR, NIS2, CRA, DORA, AI_Act]
    scope_overlap: Y   # HL engages whenever ≥2 applicable regs have ≥1 Y or Conditional-Y-scope-overlap pair on the same party; for 9.2 the matrix shows every pair has Y or Conditional-Y activation on the joint-condition operator (no N (typically) scope-disjoint pairs); the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) reflect the structural pre-launch DPIA vs continuous / iterative assessment asymmetry and stack on the layered 5-assessment architecture; the 5 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY pairs stack on distinct assessment *objects* with no consolidation rule (except the CRA↔AI_Act pair which has the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 OJ-level consolidation rule permitting substitution); the 1 SAME — EQUAL pair (NIS 2 ↔ DORA) discharges both obligations on the DORA-dominant stack for financial entities

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The high-level SO surfaces the **common documented-assessment duty** (documented assessment + identified risks + mitigation measures + review cadence) converging across GDPR Art. 35(1) + Art. 35(7) + Art. 35(11) + NIS 2 Art. 21(2)(a) + Art. 22(1) + CRA Art. 13(3) sentence 1-3 + Art. 13(7) + Annex VII §3 + DORA Art. 6(4) + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + sentence 2 + AI Act Art. 9(1) + Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) + Art. 27 FRIA; the **per-reg assessment object, trigger, content template, threshold, recipient, and review cadence** are preserved verbatim in the 5 sub-SOs. The 10 verified pairs are all SAME — 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE + 5 SAME — COMPLEMENTARY + 1 SAME — EQUAL — no CORRECTED, no CONTRADICTORY, no scope-disjoint pairs. **CRDA flag — GDPR DPIA does NOT address cybersecurity risk** (DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): for CRA-regulated products processing personal data, the GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact even when the CRA cybersecurity risk assessment has been performed; the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) reflect this structural pre-launch DPIA vs operational-continuous asymmetry. The **single OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule** — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2: `The cybersecurity risk assessment referred to in paragraph 2 shall be part of, or combined with, the risk assessment required by other Union legal acts applicable to the product with digital elements` — permits the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); no other cross-regulation substitution is OJ-permitted at 9.2. The 5 cadences are not synchronised (pre-launch / continuous / pre-market + support-period / continuous + yearly / continuous iterative); an entity must satisfy 5 independent review triggers without a unified OJ-level cadence. The **NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL pair** discharges both continuous operational risk-identification obligations on the DORA-dominant stack for financial entities under DORA's direct scope. **Most heavily-overlapped sub-domain** with **1 OJ-level consolidation rule + 0 emergent tensions**. Phase 1 entities must build a **layered 5-assessment architecture** with 5 distinct assessment objects + 5 distinct triggers + 5 distinct cadences; the HL does NOT collapse the per-reg assessment regime into a single artefact. **Step Reference:** HL requirement is decomposed into 5 per-reg sub-requirements (one per participating regulation) below; each sub-requirement carries its own derivation, fit_criterion, and applicable_if. The 5 assessment regimes operate on distinct assessment *objects* at distinct lifecycle phases; the per-reg assessment object, trigger, content template, threshold, recipient, review cadence, and evidence template are preserved verbatim in the 5 sub-SOs.
```

---

### Sub-requirements

#### 9.2.1 — Controller-side pre-launch DPIA on data-subject rights with 4-item Art. 35(7) content and Art. 35(11) on-change review

```yaml
- req_id: 9.2.1
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: integrity

  title: "Controller carries out a pre-launch DPIA covering the four Art. 35(7) content items before high-risk personal-data processing, reviewed on change of risk per Art. 35(11) and submitted for prior consultation where residual risk is high"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.2.GDPR
    per_reg_so: SO-GDPR-028 + SO-GDPR-036 + SO-GDPR-037
    source_sr: SR-GDPR-045 + SR-GDPR-046 + SR-GDPR-047
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.2.1"

  description: |
    The product shall ensure that, prior to processing likely to result in a high risk to the rights and freedoms of natural persons, the controller carries out a data-protection impact assessment (DPIA) covering the four Art. 35(7) content items (systematic description of envisaged processing + necessity-and-proportionality assessment + risks to rights and freedoms + measures envisaged to address the risks), reviewed on change of risk per Art. 35(11), and submitted for prior consultation under Art. 36(1) where residual risk remains high.

  fit_criterion: |
    A pre-launch DPIA is documented and maintained under Art. 35(1) `prior to processing … carry out an assessment of the impact of the envisaged processing operations on the protection of personal data` (SR-GDPR-045) covering the 4-item Art. 35(7) content list (SR-GDPR-046) with ID.RA-04 impact-and-likelihood dimension (Art. 35(7)(c) `assessment of the risks to the rights and freedoms of data subjects` + Art. 35(7)(d) `measures envisaged to address the risks, including safeguards, security measures and mechanisms to ensure the protection of personal data and to demonstrate compliance with this Regulation`) + ID.RA-05 threats-vulnerabilities-likelihoods-impacts dimension (Art. 35(7)(c) impact on data-subject rights and freedoms under Art. 5(1) principles); the DPIA identifies whether the processing falls within Art. 35(3)(a)-(d) mandatory-DPIA cases (systematic + extensive evaluation + large-scale special-category / systematic monitoring on large scale / innovative use of new technologies / automated decision-making with legal/similar effect) or the DPA-published Art. 35(4) blacklist; the DPIA is reviewed on change of risk per Art. 35(11) `review … where there has been a change of the risk represented by processing operations`, with material-change-of-risk trigger supplemented by a documented periodic-review cadence; the DPIA is submitted for prior consultation under Art. 36(1) where the residual risk remains high, with the controller documenting the consultation outcome in the accountability records under Art. 5(2); the DPIA is one of the records to be retained per Art. 5(2) accountability burden and is made available on request to the supervisory authority (DPA) with the DPIA content demonstrable against the 4-item Art. 35(7) list; the Art. 26 joint-controller arrangement is reflected in the DPIA with the joint-controller roles allocated per Art. 26(1) (cross to D-06.3); the Art. 35(11) review records identify the trigger event, the date, the assessment of the change, the residual-risk recalculation, and any updated measures; the controller can demonstrate Art. 5(2) accountability for the DPIA process with continuity of evidence (DPIA + reviews + prior-consultation records preserved on a continuing basis, EDPB Guidelines on accountability 07/2019); the DPIA coordinates with the DPO per Art. 39(1)(c) advice obligation (cross to 9.1 GDPR sub-SO DPO anchor); **CRDA flag — GDPR DPIA does NOT address cybersecurity risk** (DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): the DPIA's risk object is data-subject rights and freedoms; the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment or the NIS 2 Art. 21(2)(a) risk-analysis policy or the DORA Art. 7(2) ICT risk framework discharges the cybersecurity-risk-assessment obligation on a **separate artefact** — the DPIA is stacked, not collapsed with the cybersecurity assessments.

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.2.3 (when controller-manufacturer — DPIA on data-subject rights + CRA risk-assessment on product cybersecurity risk; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, distinct artefacts, no OJ-level consolidation), 9.2.4 (when DORA-controller — DPIA pre-launch + DORA continuous + yearly; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, distinct artefacts), 9.2.5 (when AI-system processes personal data — DPIA pre-launch + AI Act Art. 9 continuous iterative; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation), 9.2.2 (when controller-NSIE — DPIA pre-launch on-change + NIS 2 continuous operational; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, conditional-Y scope overlap when DPIA conducted for NSIE's processing)]

  nist_csf: [ID.RA-04, ID.RA-05]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [GDPR]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # GDPR↔NIS2 = Conditional (Y when DPIA conducted for NSIE's processing; N otherwise — pre-launch vs continuous); GDPR↔CRA = Y (CRA-regulated product processes personal data; **no OJ-level consolidation rule** — GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact even when CRA risk assessment performed; object differs — rights-and-freedoms vs cybersecurity); GDPR↔DORA = Y (DORA financial entity is controller; **no OJ-level consolidation** — DPIA pre-launch vs DORA continuous + yearly); GDPR↔AI_Act = Y (high-risk AI processes personal data; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; **no OJ-level consolidation** — DPIA pre-launch vs AI Act continuous iterative); HL engages on overlap

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The GDPR impact-and-risk-assessment obligation at 9.2 is the **pre-launch DPIA on data-subject rights** under Art. 35(1) + Art. 35(7) 4-item content + Art. 35(11) on-change review cadence. The Art. 35(3)(a)-(d) mandatory-DPIA cases and the Art. 35(4) DPA-published blacklist form the **triggering test**. Art. 35(7) 4-item content: (a) systematic description of the envisaged processing operations + purposes including legitimate interest; (b) assessment of necessity and proportionality; (c) assessment of risks to data-subject rights and freedoms; (d) measures envisaged to address the risks — the 4-item codification is the **full content template** (vs. NIS 2's entity-discretionary content under Recital 56). Art. 35(11) carries S2 VAG on `change of the risk represented by processing operations` — chosen reading: material-change-of-risk trigger supplemented by a documented periodic-review cadence. **CRDA flag — GDPR DPIA does NOT address cybersecurity risk** (DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): the DPIA's risk object is data-subject rights and freedoms under Art. 5(1); the CRA cybersecurity risk assessment, NIS 2 risk-analysis policy, DORA ICT risk framework, and AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system all operate on different objects — for a CRA-regulated product processing personal data, the GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact even when the CRA cybersecurity risk assessment has been performed; the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) preserve this structural pre-launch DPIA vs operational-continuous asymmetry without OJ-level consolidation. Art. 36(1) prior consultation is the cross-to-4.3 layer where residual risk is high. Joint-controller arrangements per Art. 26 (cross to D-06.3) are reflected in the DPIA content. The recipient is the **DPA (supervisory authority)** on Art. 35(7) DPIA content + Art. 35(11) review records + Art. 36(1) prior-consultation records + Art. 5(2) accountability burden. The DPIA coordinates with the DPO per Art. 39(1)(c) advice obligation (cross to 9.1 GDPR sub-SO DPO anchor). For a controller-NSIE (vertically-integrated entity subject to both GDPR + NIS 2), the GDPR DPIA + NIS 2 risk-analysis policy stack without OJ-level consolidation; for a controller-manufacturer (GDPR + CRA), the GDPR DPIA + CRA cybersecurity risk assessment stack without OJ-level consolidation.
```

#### 9.2.2 — Entity-level continuous operational risk-analysis policy with all-hazards methodology + Cooperation Group coordinated-assessments ingestion

```yaml
- req_id: 9.2.2
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: integrity

  title: "Essential and important entities establish and maintain documented policies on risk analysis and information system security on a continuous basis with all-hazards methodology and Cooperation Group coordinated-assessments ingestion"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.2.NIS2
    per_reg_so: SO-NIS2-022 + SO-NIS2-016
    source_sr: SR-NIS2-035 + SR-NIS2-039 + SR-NIS2-040
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.2.2"

  description: |
    The product shall ensure that the essential or important entity establishes and maintains, on a continuous basis, documented policies on risk analysis and information system security (per NIS 2 Art. 21(2)(a)) with an all-hazards methodology calibrated by the Art. 21(1) `appropriate and proportionate` chapeau and the Recital 56 proportionality factors, ingesting the EU-level Cooperation Group coordinated supply-chain risk assessments per Art. 22(1) via Art. 21(3) sentence 2.

  fit_criterion: |
    Documented policies on risk analysis and information system security are established and maintained on a continuous basis under Art. 21(2)(a) `policies on risk analysis and information system security` (SR-NIS2-035) with ID.RA-05 threats-vulnerabilities-likelihoods-impacts dimension (the entity's approach to identifying, assessing, and treating cybersecurity risks to network and information systems) + GV.OV-01 management-body oversight dimension (the policies are management-body-approved per Art. 20(1) sentence 1 first-clause `approve` + continuously-overseen per second-clause `oversees`); the policies apply an **all-hazards methodology** per SR-NIS2-040 covering cyber + non-cyber hazards (natural disasters + power failures + human errors + malicious acts + supply-chain disruptions + technical failures) with the depth calibrated by Art. 21(1) chapeau `appropriate and proportionate … taking into account the degree of exposure of the entity to risks … the likelihood of incidents … their severity` + state-of-the-art floor per Art. 21(1) sentence 2 + Recital 56 cost factor; the entity incorporates the EU-level Cooperation Group coordinated supply-chain risk assessments per Art. 22(1) into its risk-analysis policy per Art. 21(3) sentence 2 (cross to 9.4); the management body formally approves the Art. 21(2)(a) policy through a documented approval minute; the policy is reviewed and updated on a material-change-of-risk trigger (e.g., new threat landscape, organisational change, new critical-asset class, Art. 22(1) coordinated-assessment publication) with cadence entity-discretionary under Recital 56; the policy identifies the risk-analysis scope (information systems supporting essential services + business processes + supporting assets), the risk methodology (asset-threat-vulnerability-impact combination scoring, or equivalent), the risk-treatment options (accept / mitigate / transfer / avoid), the residual-risk acceptance chain, and the responsible functions; for DORA financial entities under DORA's direct scope, the DORA Art. 6(4) three-lines-of-defence + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 continuous + sentence 2 at-least-yearly discharge both DORA's dedicated continuous + at-least-yearly ICT risk-identification obligation and NIS 2's Art. 21(2)(a) continuous operational risk-analysis policy — DORA-dominant stack with EQUAL classification; the management body's Art. 20(2) sentence 1 training obligation (cross-ref 8.3.1) is the literacy prerequisite for the Art. 20(1) approve/oversee/liable architecture.

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [8.3.1 (Art. 20(2) management-body training as literacy prerequisite for Art. 20(1) approve/oversee/liable), 9.2.4 (when DORA-NSIE — DORA continuous + yearly discharges both; EQUAL, DORA-dominant), 9.2.3 (when entity procures CRA-conformant — NIS 2 entity-level operational + CRA product-level pre-market; COMPLEMENTARY; meet at supplier-customer interface — CRA risk-assessment record is an input to the NIS 2 entity's supplier-risk register, cross to 6.1), 9.2.5 (when NSIE deploys high-risk AI — NIS 2 entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; COMPLEMENTARY), 9.2.1 (when controller-NSIE — NIS 2 continuous + GDPR DPIA pre-launch on-change; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, conditional-Y scope overlap when DPIA conducted for NSIE's processing)]

  nist_csf: [ID.RA-05, GV.OV-01]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [NIS2]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # NIS2↔GDPR = Conditional (Y when DPIA is conducted for NSIE's processing); NIS2↔CRA = Y (entity procures CRA-conformant products — meet at supplier-customer interface); NIS2↔DORA = Y (DORA financial entity is NSIE — EQUAL on continuous operational risk identification; DORA-dominant stack for financial entities); NIS2↔AI_Act = Y (NSIE deploys high-risk AI; AI Act layer adds the AI-specific 4-step process to the NIS 2 risk-analysis policy); HL engages on overlap

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The NIS 2 impact-and-risk-assessment obligation at 9.2 is the **continuous operational risk-analysis policy** under Art. 21(2)(a) `policies on risk analysis and information system security` with the **all-hazards methodology** per SR-NIS2-040 and **state-of-the-art floor + cost factor proportionality calibration** per Art. 21(1) + Recital 56. Entity-discretionary content (no OJ-codified content list — vs. GDPR's 4-item Art. 35(7) or CRA's Annex VII §3) — chosen reading: the OJ-codified behaviour is the **continuous methodology** + **proportionality calibration** + **ingestion of Cooperation Group coordinated assessments** + **management-body approval**; the entity chooses the depth and the supporting methodology under Recital 56. The Art. 21(2)(a) policy is one of the Art. 21(2) sub-points established under the Art. 21(1) chapeau `appropriate and proportionate all-hazards approach`. The Art. 22(1) EU-level coordinated supply-chain risk assessments are the **Collaboration-Group-level coordinated-assessment substrate** for NSIEs to incorporate via Art. 21(3) sentence 2; the entity must document the incorporation step in the policy (cross to 9.4 for the records-of-processing layer). The **NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL classification** on continuous operational risk identification discharges both obligations on the DORA-dominant stack for financial entities under DORA's direct scope; for non-financial NSIEs, NIS 2 is the authority. The recipient is the **management body + competent authority (national NIS 2 supervisory authority)** on Art. 21(2)(a) policy + Art. 21(3) sentence 2 ingestion of Art. 22(1) coordinated assessments + Art. 20(1) management-body approval records. The **state-of-the-art floor** (Art. 21(1) sentence 2) and **cost factor** (Recital 56) constrain the entity's discretion: the methodology cannot be so lightweight that it fails to keep pace with state-of-the-art threat intelligence, and the cost factor adjusts the depth without lowering the state-of-the-art floor below the threshold of responsible practice. Member State transposition divergence may apply on the documentation expected to evidence the continuous methodology and the management-body approval.
```

#### 9.2.3 — Manufacturer-side pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment with Annex VII §3 record + subject to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation

```yaml
- req_id: 9.2.3
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: integrity

  title: "Manufacturer documents the cybersecurity risk assessment referred to in Art. 13(2) covering intended purpose + reasonably foreseeable use + Annex I Part I (2) applicability, updated during support period and recorded in Annex VII §3"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.2.CRA
    per_reg_so: SO-CRA-053 + SO-CRA-054
    source_sr: SR-CRA-064 + SR-CRA-065 + SR-CRA-066 + SR-CRA-079 + SR-CRA-080 + SR-CRA-081
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.2.3"

  description: |
    The product shall ensure that the manufacturer documents and updates, during the support period, the cybersecurity risk assessment referred to in Art. 13(2) covering intended purpose and reasonably foreseeable use (Art. 13(3) sentence 2), indicating how Annex I Part I (2) requirements apply and are implemented (Art. 13(3) sentence 3), with the assessment drawn up before placing the product on the market and updated during the support period (Art. 13(4) sentence 1 + Art. 31(2)) — documented in the Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record, and subject to the CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation rule where applicable.

  fit_criterion: |
    The cybersecurity risk assessment is documented under Art. 13(3) sentence 1 `documented and updated` (SR-CRA-064) with ID.RA-05 threats-vulnerabilities-likelihoods-impacts dimension (Art. 13(2) `cybersecurity risk assessment … taking into account the intended purpose and reasonably foreseeable use, including operational environment and assets to be protected`) + ID.SC-04 suppliers-prioritised-by-criticality dimension (the supplier-side risk-assessment perspective informing the manufacturer risk assessment, cross to 6.1); the assessment covers per Art. 13(3) sentence 2 the intended purpose + reasonably foreseeable use including operational environment and assets to be protected, with reasonable foreseeability scoped to `intentional misuses … as well as in those uses which are reasonably foreseeable by the manufacturer`; the assessment indicates per Art. 13(3) sentence 3 `whether and in what manner the Annex I Part I (2) requirements apply to the relevant product and how those requirements are implemented as informed by the risk assessment` — the applicability mapping from Annex I Part I (2) to the product's circumstances; the assessment is documented in the Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record per Art. 13(4) sentence 1 `The product with digital elements shall be accompanied by the technical documentation referred to in Annex VII` + Annex VII §3 (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); the assessment is drawn up before placing on the market and updated during the support period per Art. 13(3) sentence 1 `updated as appropriate` + Art. 13(7) `upon becoming aware of a previously unidentified vulnerability` + Art. 13(8) support period + Art. 31(2) `drawn up before placing on the market and updated during the support period`; the assessment is retained at the disposal of market surveillance authorities for at least 10 years after placement on the market or the support period, whichever is longer, per Art. 13(13); the assessment is subject to the presumption-of-conformity machinery per Art. 27(1)/(2)/(5)/(8)/(9) where harmonised standards / common specifications / European cybersecurity certification scheme under Regulation (EU) 2019/881 apply — the presumption discharges the CRA conformity-assessment obligation where alignment exists (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); **CRITICAL consolidation rule — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2**: `The cybersecurity risk assessment referred to in paragraph 2 shall be part of, or combined with, the risk assessment required by other Union legal acts applicable to the product with digital elements` — for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products (the SAME product is both CRA-regulated and AI-Act high-risk), a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment on a single artefact (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); this is the ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2 and no other cross-regulation substitution is OJ-permitted (the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when CRA cybersecurity risk assessment performed, per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175); the manufacturer can demonstrate ex post, against the market surveillance authority, that the Art. 13(3) sentence 1 documented-and-updated risk assessment was carried out with the Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record + Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping + on-material-change update per Art. 13(7) + 10-year retention per Art. 13(13).

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.2.5 (when product is both CRA-regulated and AI-Act high-risk — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation rule permits AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment on a single artefact; the ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2), 9.2.1 (when controller-manufacturer — CRA cybersecurity risk assessment on product cybersecurity risk + GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, distinct artefacts, **no OJ-level consolidation rule** even when CRA risk assessment performed — GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact), 9.2.4 (when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant — CRA product-level pre-market + DORA entity-level operational; COMPLEMENTARY, Conditional-Y scope overlap), 9.2.2 (when entity procures CRA-conformant — CRA product-level + NIS 2 entity-level operational; COMPLEMENTARY; CRA risk-assessment record is an input to NIS 2 entity's supplier-risk register, meet at supplier-customer interface)]

  nist_csf: [ID.RA-05, ID.SC-04]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [CRA]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # CRA↔GDPR = Y (CRA-regulated product processes personal data; **no OJ-level consolidation rule** — GDPR DPIA must be a separate artefact even when CRA risk assessment performed); CRA↔NIS2 = Y (entity procures CRA-conformant products; CRA product-level + NIS 2 entity-level operational; meet at supplier-customer interface); CRA↔DORA = Conditional (Y when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant; same; CRA product-level + DORA entity-level operational); CRA↔AI_Act = Y (**CRITICAL CONSOLIDATION RULE** — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 permits AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product; only OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2); HL engages on overlap

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The CRA impact-and-risk-assessment obligation at 9.2 is the **pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment** under Art. 13(3) sentence 1 `documented and updated` + sentence 2 `intended purpose and reasonably foreseeable use` + sentence 3 `Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping` + Art. 13(4) sentence 1 technical-documentation inclusion + Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation with AI Act Art. 9 + Art. 13(7) update on becoming aware of vulnerability + Art. 13(8) support period + Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record. The risk object is **product cybersecurity risk**, distinct from the GDPR DPIA's data-subject-rights object (per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175). The reasonable-foreseeability scoping under Art. 13(3) sentence 2 + the Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping under sentence 3 are the **content template** for the assessment; the substantive risk-analysis methodology is entity-discretionary under CRA's OJ. The **support-period** is determined per Art. 13(8) sentence 2 with the listed factors (reasonable expected user lifetime + nature of product + intended purpose + relevant industry standards + vendor statements). The **presumption-of-conformity machinery** per Art. 27(1)/(2)/(5)/(8)/(9) discharges the CRA conformity-assessment obligation where the assessment aligns with harmonised standards / common specifications / European cybersecurity certification schemes — but the presumption is **rebuttable** and the MSA may verify Annex I compliance on substance. **CRITICAL consolidation rule — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2** (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes): for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products, the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment on a single artefact; this is the ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2. No other cross-regulation substitution is OJ-permitted — the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when CRA risk assessment has been performed. The recipient is the **market surveillance authority (MSA)** on Art. 13(3) documented-and-updated risk assessment + Annex VII §3 risk-assessment record + Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping + Art. 13(4) sentence 1 technical-documentation inclusion + Art. 27(1)/(2)/(5)/(8)/(9) conformity-assessment records + Annex VII technical documentation (cross to 9.4 for the records-of-processing layer). The Art. 13(13) 10-year retention applies to the technical documentation including the risk-assessment record. For a controller-manufacturer (vertically-integrated entity processing personal data AND shipping CRA-regulated products), the CRA cybersecurity risk assessment on product cybersecurity risk and the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights are **distinct artefacts** with no OJ-level consolidation (per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175).
```

#### 9.2.4 — Financial-entity continuous ICT risk identification register + at-least-yearly risk-scenario review under three-lines-of-defence

```yaml
- req_id: 9.2.4
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: integrity

  title: "Financial entity continuously identifies ICT risks and reviews risk scenarios at least yearly under the three-lines-of-defence model with classification and documentation per Art. 7(1)-(2)"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.2.DORA
    per_reg_so: SO-DORA-017 + SO-DORA-003
    source_sr: SR-DORA-028 + SR-DORA-004 + SR-DORA-005
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.2.4"

  description: |
    The product shall ensure that the financial entity, on a continuous basis, identifies all sources of ICT risk and assesses cyber threats and ICT vulnerabilities relevant to its ICT supported business functions, information assets, and ICT assets (per DORA Art. 7(2) sentence 1), with risk scenarios reviewed on a regular basis and at least yearly (per Art. 7(2) sentence 2), under a three-lines-of-defence model (per Art. 6(4)) and with identification / classification / documentation per Art. 7(1).

  fit_criterion: |
    The financial entity's ICT risk identification register is maintained on a continuous basis under Art. 7(2) sentence 1 `continuously … identify all sources of ICT risk … including the risk exposure to and from other financial entities, and assess cyber threats and ICT vulnerabilities relevant to ICT supported business functions, information assets and ICT assets` (SR-DORA-028) with ID.RA-05 threats-vulnerabilities-likelihoods-impacts dimension (the register identifies ICT risk sources + exposure to/from other financial entities + cyber threats + ICT vulnerabilities, mapped to the ICT supported business functions / information assets / ICT assets identified and classified per Art. 7(1) `identify, classify and adequately document all ICT supported business functions … the information assets and ICT assets` cross-ref 9.3) + GV.RR-02 management-body approve/oversee dimension (the register is management-body-overseen per Art. 5(2) four-verb coordination `define, approve, oversee and be responsible for`); the register is operated under the **three-lines-of-defence model** per Art. 6(4) (operational-management as first line, risk-management and compliance as second line, internal-audit as third line) with responsibility for managing and overseeing ICT risk assigned to a control function with appropriate level of independence; the risk-scenario review is conducted on a regular basis and at least yearly per Art. 7(2) sentence 2 `review on a regular basis, and at least yearly, the risk scenarios impacting them`, with the depth calibrated to Art. 4 proportionality `in relation to the size, nature, scale and complexity of the financial entity's activities`; the register and review records are retained per EBA Guidelines on ICT and security risk management supervisory baseline; the register coordinates with Art. 7(1) ICT asset inventory (cross to 9.3 DORA sub-SO) and the Art. 6(1) ICT risk-management framework (cross to 9.1 DORA sub-SO); for financial entities under DORA's direct scope, the **continuous + at-least-yearly ICT risk framework** discharges both DORA's dedicated risk-identification obligation and NIS 2's Art. 21(2)(a) continuous operational risk-analysis policy on the DORA-dominant stack — EQUAL classification (per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 110-114); the management body's Art. 5(4) `actively keep up to date` training obligation (cross-ref 8.3.2) is the literacy prerequisite for the Art. 5(2) define/approve/oversee/be-responsible architecture; **DORA ICT risk framework does NOT extend to data-subject rights** (per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the DORA ICT risk framework is in place; **DORA has no equivalent to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2** — the DORA ↔ AI_Act pair has no OJ-level consolidation rule.

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [8.3.2 (Art. 5(4) management-body training as literacy prerequisite for Art. 5(2) define/approve/oversee/be-responsible), 9.2.2 (when DORA-NSIE — DORA continuous + yearly discharges both; EQUAL, DORA-dominant stack for financial entities), 9.2.1 (when DORA-controller — DPIA pre-launch + DORA continuous + yearly; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, distinct artefacts, **no OJ-level consolidation rule**), 9.2.3 (when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant — DORA entity-level operational + CRA product-level pre-market; COMPLEMENTARY, Conditional-Y scope overlap), 9.2.5 (when DORA FE deploys high-risk AI — DORA entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; COMPLEMENTARY, **no OJ-level consolidation rule**)]

  nist_csf: [ID.RA-05, GV.RR-02]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [DORA]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # DORA↔NIS2 = Y (DORA financial entity is NSIE; EQUAL on continuous operational risk identification; DORA-dominant stack for financial entities); DORA↔GDPR = Y (DORA financial entity is controller; **no OJ-level consolidation rule** — DPIA pre-launch vs DORA continuous + yearly; distinct artefacts); DORA↔CRA = Conditional (Y when DORA entity procures CRA-conformant; CRA product-level + DORA entity-level operational); DORA↔AI_Act = Y (DORA FE deploys high-risk AI; **no OJ-level consolidation rule** — DORA has no equivalent to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2); HL engages on overlap

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The DORA impact-and-risk-assessment obligation at 9.2 is the **continuous + at-least-yearly ICT risk identification + risk-scenario review** under Art. 6(4) three-lines-of-defence + Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + sentence 2. The risk object is **financial-entity ICT operational risk** (sources of ICT risk + exposure to/from other financial entities + cyber threats + ICT vulnerabilities relevant to ICT supported business functions + information assets + ICT assets per Art. 7(2) sentence 1 + Art. 7(1) identify/classify/adequately document chain, cross to 9.3 DORA sub-SO). Art. 7(2) sentence 1 `continuously … identify all sources of ICT risk` carries S2 VAG on `continuously` (chosen reading: ongoing operational frequency, not project-end); Art. 7(2) sentence 2 `review on a regular basis, and at least yearly, the risk scenarios` carries a 2-way `regular basis AND at least yearly` COORD — `at least yearly` is the OJ-minimum cadence with `regular basis` permitting additional reviews on material-change triggers. The EBA Guidelines on ICT and security risk management operationalise the supervisory baseline for the register + review records. The **NIS 2 ↔ DORA EQUAL pair** (per CRDA DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 110-114) discharges both continuous operational risk-identification obligations on the DORA-dominant stack for financial entities under DORA's direct scope; for non-financial NSIEs, NIS 2 is the authority. **DORA has no equivalent to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2** — the DORA ↔ AI_Act pair has no OJ-level consolidation rule, so a DORA financial entity deploying a CRA-conformant high-risk AI system discharges three separate assessment artefacts (DORA continuous + yearly + CRA pre-market + support-period + AI Act Art. 9 continuous iterative — with CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 permitting CRA + AI Act consolidation on a single artefact, leaving DORA separate). **DORA ICT risk framework does NOT extend to data-subject rights** (per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the DORA ICT risk framework is in place; the 4 SAME — DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE pairs (all 4 GDPR pairs) preserve this structural pre-launch DPIA vs operational-continuous asymmetry. The recipient is the **competent authority (NCA — national financial supervisory authority)** + EBA Guidelines supervisory baseline on Art. 7(2) sentence 1 continuous ICT risk identification register + sentence 2 at-least-yearly risk-scenario review + Art. 6(4) three-lines-of-defence documentation + Art. 7(1) asset chain.
```

#### 9.2.5 — Provider-side continuous iterative Art. 9 risk-management system with 4-step process + Art. 27 FRIA; subject to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation rule

```yaml
- req_id: 9.2.5
  volere_section: 15
  volere_subtype: integrity

  title: "Provider establishes, implements, documents, and maintains a continuous iterative risk-management system across the AI lifecycle with the four Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) steps, cross-coupled with Art. 27 FRIA for certain Annex III categories"

  derives_from:
    hso_sub_so: SO-9.2.AI_Act
    per_reg_so: SO-AIACT-001
    source_sr: SR-AIACT-001 + SR-AIACT-002
    hso_file: ../../HierarchicalSecurityObjectives/D-09_Governance-Documentation/9.2.md
    objective_ref_section: "### 9.2.5"

  description: |
    The product shall ensure that the provider of a high-risk AI system establishes, implements, documents, and maintains a continuous iterative risk-management system across the AI lifecycle (per AI Act Art. 9(1)) running the four Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) steps (identify/analyse known + reasonably foreseeable risks; estimate/evaluate reasonably-foreseeable-misuse risks; evaluate post-market-monitoring-data risks; adopt appropriate and targeted risk-management measures), cross-coupled with Art. 27 FRIA documentation for certain Annex III categories.

  fit_criterion: |
    A continuous iterative risk-management system is established, implemented, documented, and maintained across the AI lifecycle under Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` (SR-AIACT-001) with ID.RA-05 threats-vulnerabilities-likelihoods-impacts dimension (Art. 9(2)(a) `the identification and analysis of the known and the reasonably foreseeable risks to health, safety and fundamental rights` + Art. 9(2)(b) `the estimation and evaluation of the risks emerging from use under conditions of reasonably foreseeable misuse` + Art. 9(2)(c) `the evaluation of other risks arising from analysis of post-market monitoring data`) + GV.RM-04 strategic-direction-and-review-coupling dimension (Art. 9(2)(d) `the adoption of appropriate and targeted risk-management measures` with documented feedback into the AI lifecycle); the risk-management system runs the **4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) process**: (a) identify and analyse known and reasonably foreseeable risks to health, safety and fundamental rights; (b) estimate and evaluate risks emerging from use under conditions of reasonably foreseeable misuse (adversarial use + out-of-scope use + accidental misuse); (c) evaluate other risks arising from analysis of post-market monitoring data per Art. 72 + Art. 73 incident reporting; (d) adopt appropriate and targeted risk-management measures (elimination first where reasonably practicable, then engineering controls + protective + information measures); the system is **continuous iterative** across the AI lifecycle (design → development → placement on market → operation → post-market monitoring → significant modification → decommissioning) with documented iteration triggers and review cadences; the system is **cross-coupled** with Art. 27 FRIA for certain Annex III categories (deployer-side Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment) where the high-risk AI system is in Annex III and deployed by public authorities + private actors providing public services + banking + insurance + employment + law enforcement + migration + justice + democratic-process use cases — the FRIA documents the fundamental-rights risk dimension that complements the Art. 9 risk-management system on the deployer side; **CRITICAL consolidation rule** — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification: for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products (the SAME product is both CRA-regulated and AI-Act high-risk), a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); this is the ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2; the unified artefact must satisfy BOTH Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) + Art. 13(3) sentence 1-3 content (intended purpose + reasonably foreseeable use + Annex I Part I (2) applicability mapping); the provider can demonstrate ex post, against the market surveillance authority + AI Office, that the Art. 9(1) risk-management system was carried out across the AI lifecycle with the 4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) process documented and integrated with Art. 27 FRIA documentation for certain Annex III categories; the provider coordinates with deployer measures per Art. 26(1) `use of the high-risk AI system in accordance with the instructions for use accompanying the system` with the deployer-side Art. 4(5) + Art. 26(1) human-oversight measures layered on the provider-side risk-management measures; **AI Act risk management does NOT address data-subject rights** (per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system is in place; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation.

  priority: MUST
  verification_method: TEST
  dependencies: [9.2.3 (when product is both CRA-regulated and AI-Act high-risk — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 consolidation rule permits AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system to substitute for CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment on a single artefact; the ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2), 9.2.1 (when high-risk AI processes personal data — AI Act Art. 9 continuous iterative + GDPR DPIA pre-launch on-change; DIFFERENT-PERSPECTIVE, distinct artefacts, **no OJ-level consolidation rule**; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation), 9.2.2 (when NSIE deploys high-risk AI — NIS 2 entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; COMPLEMENTARY), 9.2.4 (when DORA FE deploys high-risk AI — DORA entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; COMPLEMENTARY, **no OJ-level consolidation rule** — DORA has no equivalent to CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2)]

  nist_csf: [ID.RA-05, GV.RM-04]

  applicable_if:
    regs: [AI_Act]
    scope_overlap: Conditional   # AI_Act↔GDPR = Y (high-risk AI processes personal data; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation; **no OJ-level consolidation rule**); AI_Act↔NIS2 = Y (NSIE deploys high-risk AI; NIS 2 entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative); AI_Act↔CRA = Y (**CRITICAL CONSOLIDATION RULE** — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2 unification rule; AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment when both apply to the same product; only OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2); AI_Act↔DORA = Y (DORA FE deploys high-risk AI; DORA entity-level operational + AI Act AI-system-level continuous iterative; **no OJ-level consolidation rule**); HL engages on overlap

  satisfaction:
    controls: []
    nodes: []

  considerations: |
    The AI Act impact-and-risk-assessment obligation at 9.2 is the **continuous iterative risk-management system** under Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` with the **4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) process** and Art. 27 FRIA for certain Annex III categories. The risk object is **AI-system risk** (health, safety, fundamental rights). Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` carries S2 features — `continuously iterative across the AI lifecycle` (chosen reading: ongoing operational frequency with documented iteration triggers); the 4-step Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) closed list is a **full content template** with sub-steps (a) known + reasonably foreseeable risks (b) reasonably foreseeable misuse risks (c) post-market-monitoring-data risks (d) appropriate + targeted risk-management measures — elimination first + engineering controls + protective + information measures per Recital 95; the 4-step process is a COORD-2 with sequencing implications — step (a) feeds (b) + (c) which together inform (d); the iterative loop feeds post-market monitoring back into step (a) per Art. 72 incident + serious-incident reporting. **Art. 27 FRIA cross-coupling** for certain Annex III categories: deployer-side Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment (Art. 27(1)) documented `before first use of the system` (Art. 27(3)) with content per Art. 27(4) — categories of data subjects + relevant fundamental-rights implications + reasonably foreseeable risks + measures to address them; cross-coupled with the provider-side Art. 9 risk-management system on the deployer side. **CRITICAL consolidation rule — CRA Art. 13(4) sentence 2**: for AI-enabled CRA-regulated products (the SAME product is both CRA-regulated and AI-Act high-risk), a unified AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system substitutes for the CRA Art. 13(3) cybersecurity risk assessment (per SR-CRA-066 ambiguity_notes); the unified artefact must satisfy BOTH Art. 9(2)(a)-(d) + Art. 13(3) sentence 1-3 content. This is the **ONLY OJ-level cross-regulation consolidation rule in 9.2** and no other cross-regulation substitution is OJ-permitted. **AI Act risk management does NOT address data-subject rights** (per CRDA flag DomainAnalysis §9.2 line 175): the GDPR DPIA on data-subject rights must be a separate artefact even when the AI Act Art. 9 risk-management system is in place; AI Act Recital 78 explicit GDPR preservation. The recipient is the **provider-supervised + market surveillance authority (MSA) + AI Office** on Art. 9(1) `established, implemented, documented and maintained` + Art. 9(2) 4-step documentation + Art. 27 FRIA documentation for certain Annex III categories + Art. 26(1) deployer-measures alignment per provider instructions for use (cross to D-13 deployer instructions). For a DORA financial entity deploying CRA-conformant high-risk AI (a vertically-integrated 5-reg stack), the high-level SO surfaces the 5-assessment architecture (GDPR pre-launch DPIA + NIS 2 continuous operational risk-analysis policy + CRA pre-market + support-period cybersecurity risk assessment consolidated with AI Act Art. 9 on a single artefact per Art. 13(4) sentence 2 + DORA continuous + at-least-yearly ICT risk framework = 4 artefacts with 1 consolidation, not 5; AI Act Art. 9 + CRA Art. 13(3) collapse onto one artefact).
```
